!Sample_geo_accession = GSM7174474
!Sample_status = Public on Oct 12 2023
!Sample_submission_date = Apr 14 2023
!Sample_last_update_date = Oct 12 2023
!Sample_type = SRA
!Sample_channel_count = 1
!Sample_source_name_ch1 = 2 Cell embryos
!Sample_organism_ch1 = Mus musculus
!Sample_taxid_ch1 = 10090
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = tissue: 2 Cell embryos
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = genotype: wild type
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = strain: C57BL/6J x  PWK/PhJ
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = chip antibody: NR5A2 (antiLRH-1/NR5A2, H2325, R&D)
!Sample_growth_protocol_ch1 = For collection of wildtypes 2 and 8C embryos, 4–6-week-old C57BL/6J female mice were injected with pregnant mare serum gonadotropin (PMSG) followed by human chorionic gonadotropin (hCG; Ningbo Hormone Product Co., Cat # 110251283 Ltd., China 10 IU) 48 hours before being mated with PWK/PhJ mice. Fertilized 2C (39-43h) and 8C (68-70h) embryos were flushed out from the oviduct with HEPES-buffered CZB medium.
!Sample_molecule_ch1 = genomic DNA
!Sample_extract_protocol_ch1 = Cells or embyos were lysed in wash buffer.
!Sample_extract_protocol_ch1 = CUT&RUN was performed as previously reported (Skene et al., 2018; Skene and Henikoff, 2017) with modifications. Briefly, after removing the zona pellucida with Tyrode’s solution (Sigma-Aldrich, T1788) or 0.5% (m/v) Protease from Streptomyces griseus (Sigma-Aldrich, P8811), early embryos were incubated with Concanavalin-coated magnetic beads (Polyscience, 86057) for 10 min at room temperature on a thermomixer at 400 rpm. Samples were then incubated with primary antibody at a ratio of 1:100 at 4℃ for 3-5 hours on a thermomixer at 400 rpm. After washing for one time, beads were incubated with pA-MNase (to a final concentration of 400-700ng/mL) (a gift from Steven Henikoff lab) at 4℃ for 1 hours on a thermomixer at 400 rpm. After two times of washing, targeted digestion was performed by adding 2μL of 100mM CaCl2 for 30 mins on ice, followed by termination by adding an equal volume of 2  × stop buffer. Samples were then incubated at 37℃ for 20 mins for fragment releasing. The total samples or supernatants were digested with Proteinase K (NEB, P8107S) and purified using phenol:chloroform:isoamyl alcohol (25:24:1, v/v) followed by ethanol purification at -80℃ overnight. The next day, DNA was purified and subjected to Truseq library preparation using NEBNext Ultra II DNA Library Prep Kit for Illumina (NEB, E7645S). Sequencing was done using the HiSeq X Ten system or  NovaSeq (Illumina) according to the manufacturer’s protocol.
!Sample_data_processing = ChIP-seq reads were mapped to the mm9 reference genome using Bowtie2 with parameters -t -q -N 1 -L 25. All ummapped reads, non-uniqurely mapped reads and PCR duplicates were removed. For downstream analysis, we computed the numbers of reads per kilobase of bin per million reads sequenced (RPKM) which were further normalized by input. A resolution of 100bp bin in genome was used in the rpkm file.
!Sample_data_processing = bulk RNA-seq reads were mapped to the mm9 reference genome using Tophat (version 2.0.11). scRNA-seq reads were mapped to mm9 genome by HISAT2 (version 2.1.0).The gene expression levels were calculated by Cufflinks (version 2.2.1). RefFlat database from UCSC genome browser was used with microRNA and repeats excluded.
!Sample_data_processing = CUT&RUN reads were aligned to the mm9 genome with the parameters: -t -q -N 1 -L 25 -X 2000 --no-mixed --no-discordant by Bowtie2 v2.3.5. Aligned reads were filtered with a minimum MAPQ of 20, and PCR duplicates were removed using Picard MarkDuplicates v1.119. Read coverages over mm9 genome were estimated by bamCoverage from deepTools v3.3.1 with parameters --binSize 100 --normalizeUsing RPKM.
!Sample_data_processing = Assembly: mm9

!Sample_growth_protocol_ch1 = serum mES cells were cultured on 0.1% gelatin of porcine skin in DMEM (Gibco Cat. 11995-065) containing 15% FBS (Hyclone Cat. SH30396.03), leukemia inhibiting factor (LIF) (Millipore Cat. GSE1107), penicillin/streptomycin (Millipore Cat. TMS-AB2-C), GlutaMAX (Gibco Cat. 35050-061), nucleotides (Millipore Cat. ES-008-D), β-mercaptoethanol (Gibco Cat. 21985-023), and non-essential amino acids (Gibco Cat. 25-025-CIR).
!Sample_molecule_ch1 = genomic DNA
!Sample_extract_protocol_ch1 = Cells or embyos were lysed in wash buffer.
!Sample_extract_protocol_ch1 = CUT&RUN was conducted following the published protocol (Skene et al., 2018). ESCs or embryos with zona pellucida removed were transferred into 0.2mL conventional, non-low-binding PCR tube (Axygen). The samples were resuspended by 60μL washing buffer (HEPES-KOH, pH = 7.5, 20mM; NaCl, 150mM; Spermidine, 0.5mM and with Roche complete protease inhibitor. Concanavalin-coated magnetic beads (Polyscience, 86057) 10-20μL for each sample were gently washed, resuspended by binding buffer (HEPES-KOH, pH = 7.9, 20mM; KCl, 10mM; CaCl2, 1mM; MnCl2, 1mM) and carefully added to the samples. The samples were then incubated at 23°C for 10 mins on Thermomixer (Eppendorf) at 400 rpm. The samples were then held at the magnetic stand to carefully aspirate buffer and were resuspended by 75μL antibody buffer (washing buffer plus 0.01% digitonin; EDTA, pH = 8.0, 2mM) with NR5A2 antibody (R&D, P-H2325-00) diluted at ratio of 1:100. The samples were then incubated at 4°C on Thermomixer for 3 hours at 400 rpm. After that, the samples were held at the magnetic stand and transfer to antibody buffer with rabbit anti-mouse IgG (Abcam, ab46540) diluted at ratio of 1:100. After that he samples were washed by 200μL dig washing buffer (washing buffer plus 0.01% digitonin) for once and 100μL for a second time. The samples were then resuspended by 75 μL dig washing buffer with pA-MNase (to a final concentration of 700ng/mL), and incubated at 4°C on Thermomixer for 1 hour at 400 rpm. The samples were washed by 200μL dig washing buffer twice at the magnetic stand. The samples were resuspended by 100μL dig washing buffer and balanced on ice for 2 mins. Targeted digestion was performed on ice by adding 2μL 100mM CaCl2 for 30 mins, and reaction was stopped by adding 100μL 2 × stop buffer (NaCl, 340mM; EDTA, pH = 8.0, 20mM; EGTA, pH = 8.0, 4mM; RNase A, 50μg/mL; glycogen, 100μg/mL) and fully vortexed. The samples were then incubated at 37°C of 30 mins for fragment releasing. The supernatants were purified by phenol-chloroform followed by ethanol purification.
!Sample_data_processing = Basecalls performed using CASAVA version 1.8
!Sample_data_processing = The paired-end reads were aligned with the parameters: -t –q –N 1 –L 25 –X 1000 --no- mixed --no-discordant by Bowtie2 (version 2.2.2) (Langmead and Salzberg, 2012). All unmapped reads, non-uniquely mapped reads, and PCR duplicates were removed. For downstream analysis, we normalized the read counts by computing the numbers of reads per kilobase of bin per million of reads sequenced (RPKM) for 100-bp bins of the genome. To minimize the batch and cell type variation, RPKM values across the whole genome were further Z-score normalized. To visualize the CUT&RUN signals in the UCSC genome browser, we generated the RPKM values on a 100bp-window base. Peaks were called using MACS v1.4.2 (Zhang et al., 2008) with the parameters nolambda –nomodel. Promoters were defined as ±2.5 kb around the transcription starting sites (TSS). Peaks at least 2.5 kb away from TSSs were defined as distal peaks by BEDTools v2.29.0 (Quinlan and Hall, 2010) .

!Sample_growth_protocol_ch1 = ES cells were cultured in non-adhesive bacterial dishes using ES culture medium minus LIF for 4 days. Then retinoic acid (RA) (Sigma Cat. R2625) was added in to medium to the final concentration of 2ug/ml. Medium was changed every day. On the 5th day of adding RA, embryonic bodies were dissociated by 0.25% Trypsin-EDTA (Gibco Cat. 25200-056) and plated to poly-L-ornithine (Sigma Cat. P3655) and Laminin (Invitrogen Cat. 23017-015) pre-coated dish. Cells were cultured in DMEM/F-12 (Gibco Cat. 11330-032) with B27 (Life tech. Cat. 17504-044) and were incubated for two days. The medium was switched to BME (Gibco, Cat. 21010-046) with B27 for another two days. Differentiated neurons were then harvested for subsequent analyses.
!Sample_molecule_ch1 = genomic DNA
!Sample_extract_protocol_ch1 = Cells were crosslinked with 1% formaldehyde for 10 min at 37◦C; then, crude nuclei were purified. Chromatin was fragmented by sonication to obtain fragments 200–2000 bp in length. For each ChIP assay, 2–5 ug of antibodies were added and incubated at 4◦C overnight. Chromatin was reverse-crosslinked, purified and quantified with a Qubit fluorometer using the Quant-iT dsDNA HS assay kit
!Sample_extract_protocol_ch1 = DNA was end-repaired, adenylated, and ligated to TruSeq sequencing adaptors. DNA was purified by AMPure beads or DNA purification kit according to the DNA amount. Library DNA was amplified by Phusion HF (NEB, Cat. M0530L). The amplified DNA was size selected using 2% agarose gel for 200-500bp DNA fragments.

For downstream analysis, we normalized the read counts by computing the numbers of reads per kilobase of bin per million of reads sequenced (RPKM) for 100-bp bins of the genome. To minimize the batch and cell type variation, RPKM values across the whole genome were further Z-score normalized. To visualize the CUT&RUN signals in the UCSC genome browser, we generated the RPKM values on a 100bp-window base. Peaks were called using MACS v1.4.2 (Zhang et al., 2008) with the parameters nolambda –nomodel. Promoters were defined as ±2.5 kb around the transcription starting sites (TSS). Peaks at least 2.5 kb away from TSSs were defined as distal peaks by BEDTools v2.29.0 (Quinlan and Hall, 2010) .

Peaks were called using MACS v1.4.2 (Zhang et al., 2008) with the parameters nolambda –nomodel. Promoters were defined as ±2.5 kb around the transcription starting sites (TSS). Peaks at least 2.5 kb away from TSSs were defined as distal peaks by BEDTools v2.29.0 (Quinlan and Hall, 2010) .

!Sample_growth_protocol_ch1 = Embryos were collected from mice induced to superovulation by injections of 5 IU of pregnant mare's serum gonadotrophin (Ningbo Sansheng Pharmaceutical Co, Ltd) and 5 IU of human chorionic gonadotrophin (Ningbo Sansheng Pharmaceutical Co, Ltd) 44-48 hours_x0000__x0000__x0000__x0000__x0000__x0000__x0000__x0000__x0000__x0000__x0000__x0000__x0000__x0000_㽝㽛므羰_x0000__x0000__x0000__x0000__x0000_
!Sample_molecule_ch1 = total RNA
!Sample_extract_protocol_ch1 = Cells or embyos were lysed in lysis buffer containing RNase inhibitor.
!Sample_extract_protocol_ch1 = The Smart-seq2 libraries of embryos were prepared as previously described (Picelli et al., 2014). Embryos were lysed in lysis buffer containing RNase inhibitor according to the user manual. The library was quantified using Qubit and Agilent 2100 before being subjected to sequencing.
!Sample_data_processing = Basecalls performed using CASAVA version 1.8
!Sample_data_processing = The paired-end reads were aligned with the parameters: -t –q –N 1 –L 25 –X 1000 --no- mixed --no-discordant by Bowtie2 (version 2.2.2) (Langmead and Salzberg, 2012). All unmapped reads, non-uniquely mapped reads, and PCR duplicates were removed. For downstream analysis, we normalized the read counts by computing the numbers of reads per kilobase of bin per million of reads sequenced (RPKM) for 100-bp bins of the genome. To minimize the batch and cell type variation, RPKM values across the whole genome were further Z-score normalized. To visualize the CUT&RUN signals in the UCSC genome browser, we generated the RPKM values on a 100bp-window base. Peaks were called using MACS v1.4.2 (Zhang et al., 2008) with the parameters nolambda –nomodel. Promoters were defined as ±2.5 kb around the transcription starting sites (TSS). Peaks at least 2.5 kb away from TSSs were defined as distal peaks by BEDTools v2.29.0 (Quinlan and Hall, 2010) .

!Sample_data_processing = Basecalls performed using CASAVA version 1.8
!Sample_data_processing = The paired-end reads were aligned with the parameters: -t –q –N 1 –L 25 –X 1000 --no- mixed --no-discordant by Bowtie2 (version 2.2.2) (Langmead and Salzberg, 2012). All unmapped reads, non-uniquely mapped reads, and PCR duplicates were removed. For downstream analysis, we normalized the read counts by computing the numbers of reads per kilobase of bin per million of reads sequenced (RPKM) for 100-bp bins of the genome. To minimize the batch and cell type variation, RPKM values across the whole genome were further Z-score normalized. To visualize the CUT&RUN signals in the UCSC genome browser, we generated the RPKM values on a 100bp-window base. Peaks were called using MACS v1.4.2 (Zhang et al., 2008) with the parameters nolambda –nomodel. Promoters were defined as ±2.5 kb around the transcription starting sites (TSS). Peaks at least 2.5 kb away from TSSs were defined as distal peaks by BEDTools v2.29.0 (Quinlan and Hall, 2010) .

!Sample_geo_accession = GSM7922939

!Sample_type = SRA

!Sample_source_name_ch1 = Preimplantation embryo
!Sample_organism_ch1 = Mus musculus
!Sample_taxid_ch1 = 10090
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = genotype: wild type
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = developmental stage: 8-cell
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = antibody: none
!Sample_treatment_protocol_ch1 = Injected with 5 μM  Nr5a2 siRNA separately
!Sample_growth_protocol_ch1 = Embryos were collected from mice induced to superovulation by injections of 5 IU of pregnant mare's serum gonadotrophin (Ningbo Sansheng Pharmaceutical Co, Ltd) and 5 IU of human chorionic gonadotrophin (Ningbo Sansheng Pharmaceutical Co, Ltd) 44-48 hours apart and mated to males.
!Sample_molecule_ch1 = total RNA
!Sample_extract_protocol_ch1 = Cells or embyos were lysed in lysis buffer containing RNase inhibitor.
!Sample_extract_protocol_ch1 = The Smart-seq2 libraries of embryos were prepared as previously described (Picelli et al., 2014). Embryos were lysed in lysis buffer containing RNase inhibitor according to the user manual. The library was quantified using Qubit and Agilent 2100 before being subjected to sequencing.
!Sample_data_processing = Basecalls performed using CASAVA version 1.8
!Sample_data_processing = The paired-end reads were aligned with the parameters: -t –q –N 1 –L 25 –X 1000 --no- mixed --no-discordant by Bowtie2 (version 2.2.2) (Langmead and Salzberg, 2012). All unmapped reads, non-uniquely mapped reads, and PCR duplicates were removed. For downstream analysis, we normalized the read counts by computing the numbers of reads per kilobase of bin per million of reads sequenced (RPKM) for 100-bp bins of the genome. To minimize the batch and cell type variation, RPKM values across the whole genome were further Z-score normalized. To visualize the CUT&RUN signals in the UCSC genome browser, we generated the RPKM values on a 100bp-window base. Peaks were called using MACS v1.4.2 (Zhang et al., 2008) with the parameters nolambda –nomodel. Promoters were defined as ±2.5 kb around the transcription starting sites (TSS). Peaks at least 2.5 kb away from TSSs were defined as distal peaks by BEDTools v2.29.0 (Quinlan and Hall, 2010) .

!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = genotype: wild type
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = developmental stage: 2-cell
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = antibody: none
!Sample_treatment_protocol_ch1 = Injected with 20 μM NC siRNA
!Sample_growth_protocol_ch1 = Embryos were collected from mice induced to superovulation by injections of 5 IU of pregnant mare's serum gonadotrophin (Ningbo Sansheng Pharmaceutical Co, Ltd) and 5 IU of human chorionic gonadotrophin (Ningbo Sansheng Pharmaceutical Co, Ltd) 44-48 hours apart and mated to males.
!Sample_molecule_ch1 = total RNA
!Sample_extract_protocol_ch1 = Cells or embyos were lysed in lysis buffer containing RNase inhibitor.
!Sample_extract_protocol_ch1 = The Smart-seq2 libraries of embryos were prepared as previously described (Picelli et al., 2014). Embryos were lysed in lysis buffer containing RNase inhibitor according to the user manual. The library was quantified using Qubit and Agilent 2100 before being subjected to sequencing.
!Sample_data_processing = Basecalls performed using CASAVA version 1.8
!Sample_data_processing = The paired-end reads were aligned with the parameters: -t –q –N 1 –L 25 –X 1000 --no- mixed --no-discordant by Bowtie2 (version 2.2.2) (Langmead and Salzberg, 2012). All unmapped reads, non-uniquely mapped reads, and PCR duplicates were removed. For downstream analysis, we normalized the read counts by computing the numbers of reads per kilobase of bin per million of reads sequenced (RPKM) for 100-bp bins of the genome. To minimize the batch and cell type variation, RPKM values across the whole genome were further Z-score normalized. To visualize the CUT&RUN signals in the UCSC genome browser, we generated the RPKM values on a 100bp-window base. Peaks were called using MACS v1.4.2 (Zhang et al., 2008) with the parameters nolambda –nomodel. Promoters were defined as ±2.5 kb around the transcription starting sites (TSS). Peaks at least 2.5 kb away from TSSs were defined as distal peaks by BEDTools v2.29.0 (Quinlan and Hall, 2010) .

!Sample_geo_accession = GSM7922950
!Sample_status = Public on Nov 30 2023

!Sample_type = SRA
!Sample_channel_count = 1
!Sample_source_name_ch1 = Preimplantation embryo
!Sample_organism_ch1 = Mus musculus
!Sample_taxid_ch1 = 10090
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = genotype: wild type
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = developmental stage: 2-cell
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = antibody: none
!Sample_treatment_protocol_ch1 = Injected with 5 μM NC siRNA
!Sample_growth_protocol_ch1 = Embryos were collected from mice induced to superovulation by injections of 5 IU of pregnant mare's serum gonadotrophin (Ningbo Sansheng Pharmaceutical Co, Ltd) and 5 IU of human chorionic gonadotrophin (Ningbo Sansheng Pharmaceutical Co, Ltd) 44-48 hours apart and mated to males.
!Sample_molecule_ch1 = genomic DNA
!Sample_extract_protocol_ch1 = Cells or embyos were lysed in wash buffer.
!Sample_extract_protocol_ch1 = The ATAC-seq procedure was performed as previously described (Wu et al. 2018). Briefly, cells were transferred into 6 μl of lysis buffer [10 mM tris-HCl (pH 7.4), 10 mM NaCl, 3 mM MgCl2, and 0.02% digitonin) in ice for 10 min. The ATAC reaction was performed by adding 4 μl of ddH2O, 4 μl of 5× TTBL, and 5 μl TTE mix V5 (Vazyme Biotech Co.,Ltd, catalog no. TD502) at 37°C for 30 min and then stopped by adding 5 μl of 5× TS stop buffer at room temperature for 5 min. DNA was extracted by phenol chloroform and ethanol purification after adding 40 ng of carrier RNA and 103 μl of tris-EDTA. Then, DNA was PCR-amplified with 10 μl of index (Vazyme Biotech Co.,Ltd, catalog no. TD202), 10 μl of 5× TAB and 1 μl tris-acetate-EDTA (Vazyme Biotech Co.,Ltd, catalog no. TD502) with the program of 72°C for 3 min, 98°C for 30 s, (98°C for 15 s, 60°C for 30 s, and 72°C for 3 min) with 18 cycles, and 72°C for 5 min.
!Sample_data_processing = Basecalls performed using CASAVA version 1.8
!Sample_data_processing = The paired-end reads were aligned with the parameters: -t –q –N 1 –L 25 –X 1000 --no- mixed --no-discordant by Bowtie2 (version 2.2.2) (Langmead and Salzberg, 2012). All unmapped reads, non-uniquely mapped reads, and PCR duplicates were removed. For downstream analysis, we normalized the read counts by computing the numbers of reads per kilobase of bin per million of reads sequenced (RPKM) for 100-bp bins of the genome. To minimize the batch and cell type variation, RPKM values across the whole genome were further Z-score normalized. To visualize the CUT&RUN signals in the UCSC genome browser, we generated the RPKM values on a 100bp-window base. Peaks were called using MACS v1.4.2 (Zhang et al., 2008) with the parameters nolambda –nomodel. Promoters were defined as ±2.5 kb around the transcription starting sites (TSS). Peaks at least 2.5 kb away from TSSs were defined as distal peaks by BEDTools v2.29.0 (Quinlan and Hall, 2010) .

!Sample_growth_protocol_ch1 = Embryos were collected from mice induced to superovulation by injections of 5 IU of pregnant mare's serum gonadotrophin (Ningbo Sansheng Pharmaceutical Co, Ltd) and 5 IU of human chorionic gonadotrophin (Ningbo Sansheng Pharmaceutical Co, Ltd) 44-48 hours apart and mated to males.
!Sample_molecule_ch1 = genomic DNA
!Sample_extract_protocol_ch1 = Cells or embyos were lysed in wash buffer.
!Sample_extract_protocol_ch1 = The ATAC-seq procedure was performed as previously described (Wu et al. 2018). Briefly, cells were transferred into 6 μl of lysis buffer [10 mM tris-HCl (pH 7.4), 10 mM NaCl, 3 mM MgCl2, and 0.02% digitonin) in ice for 10 min. The ATAC reaction was performed by adding 4 μl of ddH2O, 4 μl of 5× TTBL, and 5 μl TTE mix V5 (Vazyme Biotech Co.,Ltd, catalog no. TD502) at 37°C for 30 min and then stopped by adding 5 μl of 5× TS stop buffer at room temperature for 5 min. DNA was extracted by phenol chloroform and ethanol purification after adding 40 ng of carrier RNA and 103 μl of tris-EDTA. Then, DNA was PCR-amplified with 10 μl of index (Vazyme Biotech Co.,Ltd, catalog no. TD202), 10 μl of 5× TAB and 1 μl tris-acetate-EDTA (Vazyme Biotech Co.,Ltd, catalog no. TD502) with the program of 72°C for 3 min, 98°C for 30 s, (98°C for 15 s, 60°C for 30 s, and 72°C for 3 min) with 18 cycles, and 72°C for 5 min.
!Sample_data_processing = Basecalls performed using CASAVA version 1.8
!Sample_data_processing = The paired-end reads were aligned with the parameters: -t –q –N 1 –L 25 –X 1000 --no- mixed --no-discordant by Bowtie2 (version 2.2.2) (Langmead and Salzberg, 2012). All unmapped reads, non-uniquely mapped reads, and PCR duplicates were removed. For downstream analysis, we normalized the read counts by computing the numbers of reads per kilobase of bin per million of reads sequenced (RPKM) for 100-bp bins of the genome. To minimize the batch and cell type variation, RPKM values across the whole genome were further Z-score normalized. To visualize the CUT&RUN signals in the UCSC genome browser, we generated the RPKM values on a 100bp-window base. Peaks were called using MACS v1.4.2 (Zhang et al., 2008) with the parameters nolambda –nomodel. Promoters were defined as ±2.5 kb around the transcription starting sites (TSS). Peaks at least 2.5 kb away from TSSs were defined as distal peaks by BEDTools v2.29.0 (Quinlan and Hall, 2010) .

TD502) with the program of 72°C for 3 min, 98°C for 30 s, (98°C for 15 s, 60°C for 30 s, and 72°C for 3 min) with 18 cycles, and 72°C for 5 min.
!Sample_data_processing = Basecalls performed using CASAVA version 1.8
!Sample_data_processing = The paired-end reads were aligned with the parameters: -t –q –N 1 –L 25 –X 1000 --no- mixed --no-discordant by Bowtie2 (version 2.2.2) (Langmead and Salzberg, 2012). All unmapped reads, non-uniquely mapped reads, and PCR duplicates were removed. For downstream analysis, we normalized the read counts by computing the numbers of reads per kilobase of bin per million of reads sequenced (RPKM) for 100-bp bins of the genome. To minimize the batch and cell type variation, RPKM values across the whole genome were further Z-score normalized. To visualize the CUT&RUN signals in the UCSC genome browser, we generated the RPKM values on a 100bp-window base. Peaks were called using MACS v1.4.2 (Zhang et al., 2008) with the parameters nolambda –nomodel. Promoters were defined as ±2.5 kb around the transcription starting sites (TSS). Peaks at least 2.5 kb away from TSSs were defined as distal peaks by BEDTools v2.29.0 (Quinlan and Hall, 2010) .

!Sample_source_name_ch1 = Preimplantation embryo
!Sample_organism_ch1 = Mus musculus
!Sample_taxid_ch1 = 10090
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = genotype: wild type
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = developmental stage: 2-cell
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = antibody: none
!Sample_treatment_protocol_ch1 = Injected with 100 ng/μl BE mRNA
!Sample_growth_protocol_ch1 = Embryos were collected from mice induced to superovulation by injections of 5 IU of pregnant mare's serum gonadotrophin (Ningbo Sansheng Pharmaceutical Co, Ltd) and 5 IU of human chorionic gonadotrophin (Ningbo Sansheng Pharmaceutical Co, Ltd) 44-48 hours apart and mated to males.
!Sample_molecule_ch1 = total RNA
!Sample_extract_protocol_ch1 = Cells or embyos were lysed in lysis buffer containing RNase inhibitor.
!Sample_extract_protocol_ch1 = The Smart-seq2 libraries of embryos were prepared as previously described (Picelli et al., 2014). Embryos were lysed in lysis buffer containing RNase inhibitor according to the user manual. The library was quantified using Qubit and Agilent 2100 before being subjected to sequencing.
!Sample_data_processing = Basecalls performed using CASAVA version 1.8
!Sample_data_processing = The paired-end reads were aligned with the parameters: -t –q –N 1 –L 25 –X 1000 --no- mixed --no-discordant by Bowtie2 (version 2.2.2) (Langmead and Salzberg, 2012). All unmapped reads, non-uniquely mapped reads, and PCR duplicates were removed. For downstream analysis, we normalized the read counts by computing the numbers of reads per kilobase of bin per million of reads sequenced (RPKM) for 100-bp bins of the genome. To minimize the batch and cell type variation, RPKM values across the whole genome were further Z-score normalized. To visualize the CUT&RUN signals in the UCSC genome browser, we generated the RPKM values on a 100bp-window base. Peaks were called using MACS v1.4.2 (Zhang et al., 2008) with the parameters nolambda –nomodel. Promoters were defined as ±2.5 kb around the transcription starting sites (TSS). Peaks at least 2.5 kb away from TSSs were defined as distal peaks by BEDTools v2.29.0 (Quinlan and Hall, 2010) .
!Sample_data_processing = Assembly: mm9

!Sample_type = SRA
!Sample_channel_count = 1
!Sample_source_name_ch1 = Preimplantation embryo
!Sample_organism_ch1 = Mus musculus
!Sample_taxid_ch1 = 10090
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = genotype: wild type
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = developmental stage: 2-cell
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = antibody: none
!Sample_treatment_protocol_ch1 = Injected with 100 ng/μl Nr5a2 sgRNA
!Sample_growth_protocol_ch1 = Embryos were collected from mice induced to superovulation by injections of 5 IU of pregnant mare's serum gonadotrophin (Ningbo Sansheng Pharmaceutical Co, Ltd) and 5 IU of human chorionic gonadotrophin (Ningbo Sansheng Pharmaceutical Co, Ltd) 44-48 hours apart and mated to males.
!Sample_molecule_ch1 = total RNA
!Sample_extract_protocol_ch1 = Cells or embyos were lysed in lysis buffer containing RNase inhibitor.
!Sample_extract_protocol_ch1 = The Smart-seq2 libraries of embryos were prepared as previously described (Picelli et al., 2014). Embryos were lysed in lysis buffer containing RNase inhibitor according to the user manual. The library was quantified using Qubit and Agilent 2100 before being subjected to sequencing.
!Sample_data_processing = Basecalls performed using CASAVA version 1.8
!Sample_data_processing = The paired-end reads were aligned with the parameters: -t –q –N 1 –L 25 –X 1000 --no- mixed --no-discordant by Bowtie2 (version 2.2.2) (Langmead and Salzberg, 2012). All unmapped reads, non-uniquely mapped reads, and PCR duplicates were removed. For downstream analysis, we normalized the read counts by computing the numbers of reads per kilobase of bin per million of reads sequenced (RPKM) for 100-bp bins of the genome. To minimize the batch and cell type variation, RPKM values across the whole genome were further Z-score normalized. To visualize the CUT&RUN signals in the UCSC genome browser, we generated the RPKM values on a 100bp-window base. Peaks were called using MACS v1.4.2 (Zhang et al., 2008) with the parameters nolambda –nomodel. Promoters were defined as ±2.5 kb around the transcription starting sites (TSS). Peaks at least 2.5 kb away from TSSs were defined as distal peaks by BEDTools v2.29.0 (Quinlan and Hall, 2010) .

Peaks were called using MACS v1.4.2 (Zhang et al., 2008) with the parameters nolambda –nomodel. Promoters were defined as ±2.5 kb around the transcription starting sites (TSS). Peaks at least 2.5 kb away from TSSs were defined as distal peaks by BEDTools v2.29.0 (Quinlan and Hall, 2010) .

!Sample_data_processing = Basecalls performed using CASAVA version 1.8
!Sample_data_processing = The paired-end reads were aligned with the parameters: -t –q –N 1 –L 25 –X 1000 --no- mixed --no-discordant by Bowtie2 (version 2.2.2) (Langmead and Salzberg, 2012). All unmapped reads, non-uniquely mapped reads, and PCR duplicates were removed. For downstream analysis, we normalized the read counts by computing the numbers of reads per kilobase of bin per million of reads sequenced (RPKM) for 100-bp bins of the genome. !Sample_extract_protocol_ch1 = Cells or embyos were lysed in lysis buffer containing RNase inhibitor.
!Sample_extract_protocol_ch1 = The Smart-seq2 libraries of embryos were prepared as previously described (Picelli et al., 2014). Embryos were lysed in lysis buffer containing RNase inhibitor according to the user manual. The library was quantified using Qubit and Agilent 2100 before being subjected to sequencing.
!Sample_data_processing = Basecalls performed using CASAVA version 1.8
!Sample_data_processing = The paired-end reads were aligned with the parameters: -t –q –N 1 –L 25 –X 1000 --no- mixed --no-discordant by Bowtie2 (version 2.2.2) (Langmead and Salzberg, 2012). All unmapped reads, non-uniquely mapped reads, and PCR duplicates were removed. For downstream analysis, we normalized the read counts by computing the numbers of reads per kilobase of bin per million of reads sequenced (RPKM) for 100-bp bins of the genome. To minimize the batch and cell type variation, RPKM values across the whole genome were further Z-score normalized. To visualize the CUT&RUN signals in the UCSC genome browser, we generated the RPKM values on a 100bp-window base. Peaks were called using MACS v1.4.2 (Zhang et al., 2008) with the parameters nolambda –nomodel. Promoters were defined as ±2.5 kb around the transcription starting sites (TSS). Peaks at least 2.5 kb away from TSSs were defined as distal peaks by BEDTools v2.29.0 (Quinlan and Hall, 2010) .

!Sample_last_update_date = Nov 30 2023
!Sample_type = SRA
!Sample_channel_count = 1
!Sample_source_name_ch1 = Preimplantation embryo
!Sample_organism_ch1 = Mus musculus
!Sample_taxid_ch1 = 10090
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = genotype: wild type
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = developmental stage: 8-cell
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = antibody: none
!Sample_treatment_protocol_ch1 = Injected with 100 ng/μl Nr5a2 sgRNA
!Sample_growth_protocol_ch1 = Embryos were collected from mice induced to superovulation by injections of 5 IU of pregnant mare's serum gonadotrophin (Ningbo Sansheng Pharmaceutical Co, Ltd) and 5 IU of human chorionic gonadotrophin (Ningbo Sansheng Pharmaceutical Co, Ltd) 44-48 hours apart and mated to males.
!Sample_molecule_ch1 = total RNA
!Sample_extract_protocol_ch1 = Cells or embyos were lysed in lysis buffer containing RNase inhibitor.
!Sample_extract_protocol_ch1 = The Smart-seq2 libraries of embryos were prepared as previously described (Picelli et al., 2014). Embryos were lysed in lysis buffer containing RNase inhibitor according to the user manual. The library was quantified using Qubit and Agilent 2100 before being subjected to sequencing.
!Sample_data_processing = Basecalls performed using CASAVA version 1.8
!Sample_data_processing = The paired-end reads were aligned with the parameters: -t –q –N 1 –L 25 –X 1000 --no- mixed --no-discordant by Bowtie2 (version 2.2.2) (Langmead and Salzberg, 2012). All unmapped reads, non-uniquely mapped reads, and PCR duplicates were removed. For downstream analysis, we normalized the read counts by computing the numbers of reads per kilobase of bin per million of reads sequenced (RPKM) for 100-bp bins of the genome. To minimize the batch and cell type variation, RPKM values across the whole genome were further Z-score normalized. To visualize the CUT&RUN signals in the UCSC genome browser, we generated the RPKM values on a 100bp-window base. Peaks were called using MACS v1.4.2 (Zhang et al., 2008) with the parameters nolambda –nomodel. Promoters were defined as ±2.5 kb around the transcription starting sites (TSS). Peaks at least 2.5 kb away from TSSs were defined as distal peaks by BEDTools v2.29.0 (Quinlan and Hall, 2010) .

!Sample_relation = BioSample: https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/biosample/SAMN38500268
!Sample_relation = SRA: https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/sra?term=SRX22680912
!Sample_supplementary_file_1 = ftp://ftp.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/geo/samples/GSM7923nnn/GSM7923005/suppl/GSM7923005_8C_sg_RNA_rep8_fpkm.txt.gz
!Sample_series_id = GSE229740
!Sample_data_row_count = 0
^SAMPLE = GSM7923006
!Sample_title = 8C_BE_sg_RNA_rep1
!Sample_geo_accession = GSM7923006
!Sample_status = Public on Nov 30 2023
!Sample_submission_date = Nov 29 2023
!Sample_last_update_date = Nov 30 2023
!Sample_type = SRA
!Sample_channel_count = 1
!Sample_source_name_ch1 = Preimplantation embryo
!Sample_organism_ch1 = Mus musculus
!Sample_taxid_ch1 = 10090
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = genotype: wild type
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = developmental stage: 8-cell
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = antibody: none
!Sample_treatment_protocol_ch1 = Injected with 100 ng/μl BE mRNA and Nr5a2 sgRNA
!Sample_growth_protocol_ch1 = Embryos were collected from mice induced to superovulation by injections of 5 IU of pregnant mare's serum gonadotrophin (Ningbo Sansheng Pharmaceutical Co, Ltd) and 5 IU of human chorionic gonadotrophin (Ningbo Sansheng Pharmaceutical Co, Ltd) 44-48 hours apart and mated to males.
!Sample_molecule_ch1 = total RNA
!Sample_extract_protocol_ch1 = Cells or embyos were lysed in lysis buffer containing RNase inhibitor.
!Sample_extract_protocol_ch1 = The Smart-seq2 libraries of embryos were prepared as previously described (Picelli et al., 2014). Embryos were lysed in lysis buffer containing RNase inhibitor according to the user manual. The library was quantified using Qubit and Agilent 2100 before being subjected to sequencing.
!Sample_data_processing = Basecalls performed using CASAVA version 1.8
!Sample_data_processing = The paired-end reads were aligned with the parameters: -t –q –N 1 –L 25 –X 1000 --no- mixed --no-discordant by Bowtie2 (version 2.2.2) (Langmead and Salzberg, 2012). All unmapped reads, non-uniquely mapped reads, and PCR duplicates were removed. For downstream analysis, we normalized the read counts by computing the numbers of reads per kilobase of bin per million of reads sequenced (RPKM) for 100-bp bins of the genome. To minimize the batch and cell type variation, RPKM values across the whole genome were further Z-score normalized. To visualize the CUT&RUN signals in the UCSC genome browser, we generated the RPKM values on a 100bp-window base. Peaks were called using MACS v1.4.2 (Zhang et al., 2008) with the parameters nolambda –nomodel. Promoters were defined as ±2.5 kb around the transcription starting sites (TSS). Peaks at least 2.5 kb away from TSSs were defined as distal peaks by BEDTools v2.29.0 (Quinlan and Hall, 2010) .

To minimize the batch and cell type variation, RPKM values across the whole genome were further Z-score normalized. To visualize the CUT&RUN signals in the UCSC genome browser, we generated the RPKM values on a 100bp-window base. Peaks were called using MACS v1.4.2 (Zhang et al., 2008) with the parameters nolambda –nomodel. Promoters were defined as ±2.5 kb around the transcription starting sites (TSS). Peaks at least 2.5 kb away from TSSs were defined as distal peaks by BEDTools v2.29.0 (Quinlan and Hall, 2010) .

!Sample_extract_protocol_ch1 = The Smart-seq2 libraries of embryos were prepared as previously described (Picelli et al., 2014). !Sample_data_processing = Basecalls performed using CASAVA version 1.8
!Sample_data_processing = The paired-end reads were aligned with the parameters: -t –q –N 1 –L 25 –X 1000 --no- mixed --no-discordant by Bowtie2 (version 2.2.2) (Langmead and Salzberg, 2012). All unmapped reads, non-uniquely mapped reads, and PCR duplicates were removed. For downstream analysis, we normalized the read counts by computing the numbers of reads per kilobase of bin per million of reads sequenced (RPKM) for 100-bp bins of the genome.
